    11→---
    12→# Lightdash セルフホスト アーキテクチャ
    13→
    14→## 概要
    15→
    16→Lightdash は **dbt ネイティブの OSS BI ツール**。dbt の `manifest.json` を読み込み、モデル定義をそのままディメンション/メトリクスとして可視化に使える。セルフホスト構成は **Cloudflare Containers + Supabase PostgreSQL + R2** で実現する。
    17→
    18→データウェアハウスは **DuckDB on R2 (Parquet)** をメインに据え、**Snowflake / Databricks をリカバリ用**として保持する設計。
    19→
    20→## アーキテクチャ全体像
    21→
    22→```mermaid
    23→graph TB
    24→    subgraph USER["ユーザー"]
    25→        Browser[ブラウザ<br/>:8080]
    26→    end
    27→
    28→    subgraph CF["Cloudflare"]
    29→        subgraph Containers["Containers"]
    30→            LD[Lightdash<br/>API + Scheduler<br/>Express + Graphile Worker]
    31→            HB[Headless Browser<br/>PDF/画像生成<br/>:3001]
    32→        end
    33→        R2[R2<br/>S3 互換ストレージ]
    34→        R2data[R2<br/>Parquet データレイク]
    35→        DNS[DNS + CDN<br/>リバースプロキシ]
    36→    end
    37→
    38→    subgraph SUPA["Supabase (外部)"]
    39→        PG[(PostgreSQL<br/>Direct 接続<br/>Free: 7日無活動で一時停止)]
    40→    end
    41→
    42→    subgraph DWH["データウェアハウス"]
    43→        DUCK[DuckDB<br/>ローカル .duckdb ファイル<br/>メインウェアハウス]
    44→        SF[Snowflake<br/>リカバリ用]
    45→        DB[Databricks<br/>リカバリ用]
    46→    end
    47→
    48→    subgraph DBT["dbt プロジェクト"]
    49→        Manifest[manifest.json<br/>dbt-duckdb アダプター]
    50→    end
    51→
    52→    Browser -->|HTTPS| DNS
    53→    DNS --> LD
    54→    LD -->|TCP :5432<br/>LISTEN/NOTIFY| PG
    55→    LD -->|S3 API| R2
    56→    LD -->|SQL クエリ| DUCK
    57→    LD -.->|フォールバック| SF
    58→    LD -.->|フォールバック| DB
    59→    LD -->|HTTP :3001| HB
    60→    R2data -->|dbt-duckdb が httpfs で読み取り<br/>ローカル .duckdb にマテリアライズ| DUCK
    61→    Manifest -->|lightdash deploy| LD
    62→```
    63→
    64→## コンポーネント詳細
    65→
    66→| コンポーネント | ホスト先 | 役割 | 備考 |
    67→|--------------|---------|------|------|
    68→| **Lightdash** | Cloudflare Containers | API サーバー + スケジューラー | `lightdash/lightdash:latest` (Docker Hub) |
    69→| **Headless Browser** | Cloudflare Containers | PDF/画像レンダリング | スケジュール配信を使わないなら省略可 |
    70→| **PostgreSQL** | **Supabase** (外部唯一) | メタデータ + Graphile Worker ジョブキュー | Direct 接続、Free は 7日無活動で一時停止 |
    71→| **R2 (エクスポート)** | Cloudflare R2 | CSV/XLSX エクスポート、スクリーンショット | S3 互換 API |
    72→| **R2 (データレイク)** | Cloudflare R2 | Parquet 形式のソースデータ | dbt-duckdb が httpfs で読み取り |
    73→| **DuckDB** | Containers 内 | メインウェアハウス | dbt-duckdb が R2 → ローカル .duckdb にマテリアライズ。Lightdash はこのファイルに接続 |
    74→| **Snowflake** | 外部 | リカバリ用ウェアハウス | DuckDB で処理不能時のフォールバック |
    75→| **Databricks** | 外部 | リカバリ用ウェアハウス | 同上 |
    76→| **DNS + CDN** | Cloudflare | リバースプロキシ、キャッシュ | |
    77→
    78→## DuckDB ウェアハウス — 現状と課題
    79→
    80→> **注意 (2026-03-31 時点)**: Lightdash は DuckDB を**まだ正式サポートしていない**。
    81→> [PR #19866](https://github.com/lightdash/lightdash/pull/19866) が Open 状態で開発中。
    82→> [Issue #11112](https://github.com/lightdash/lightdash/issues/11112) に 43 リアクション。
    83→
    84→### PR マージ前の暫定構成
    85→
    86→DuckDB PR がマージされるまでは、**Supabase PostgreSQL をウェアハウスとしても兼用**する:
    87→
    88→```
    89→Supabase PostgreSQL ─── メタデータ DB (Graphile Worker)
    90→       └────────────── ウェアハウス兼用 (暫定)
    91→
    92→R2 Parquet ──────────── dbt-duckdb でローカル変換 → Supabase にロード
    93→```
    94→
    95→### PR マージ後のターゲット構成
    96→
    97→DuckDB がサポートされたら、ウェアハウスを Supabase から DuckDB に切り替え:
    98→
    99→```
   100→Supabase PostgreSQL ─── メタデータ DB のみ
   101→
   102→R2 Parquet ─────── dbt-duckdb が httpfs で読み取り
   103→       └──────── ローカル .duckdb ファイルにマテリアライズ
   104→                       └──── Lightdash が .duckdb ファイルに接続してクエリ
   105→```
   106→
   107→> **注意**: Lightdash の DuckDB コネクター (PR #19866) は「ローカル `.duckdb` ファイルへの接続」を実装する。
   108→> R2 Parquet への httpfs 直接クエリは **dbt-duckdb 側の役割**であり、Lightdash は dbt がマテリアライズ済みの
   109→> `.duckdb` ファイルを読むだけ。この二段構成を理解しておくことが重要。
   110→
   111→### dbt-duckdb プロファイル設定 (R2 向け)
   112→
   113→DuckDB の R2 Secret Type を使う方式（推奨）:
   114→
   115→```yaml
   116→# profiles.yml
   117→default:
   118→  outputs:
   119→    dev:
   120→      type: duckdb
   121→      path: /tmp/dbt.duckdb
   122→      extensions:
   123→        - httpfs
   124→        - parquet
   125→      secrets:
   126→        - type: r2
   127→          key_id: "{{ env_var('R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID') }}"
   128→          secret: "{{ env_var('R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY') }}"
   129→          account_id: "{{ env_var('CF_ACCOUNT_ID') }}"
   130→  target: dev
   131→```
   132→
   133→fsspec 方式（代替）:
   134→
   135→```yaml
   136→default:
   137→  outputs:
   138→    dev:
   139→      type: duckdb
   140→      path: /tmp/dbt.duckdb
   141→      extensions:
   142→        - httpfs
   143→        - parquet
   144→      filesystems:
   145→        - fs: s3
   146→          anon: false
   147→          key: "{{ env_var('R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID') }}"
   148→          secret: "{{ env_var('R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY') }}"
   149→          client_kwargs:
   150→            endpoint_url: "https://{{ env_var('CF_ACCOUNT_ID') }}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com"
   151→  target: dev
   152→```
   153→
   154→### dbt ソース定義 (R2 Parquet 直接参照)
   155→
   156→```yaml
   157→# models/sources.yml
   158→sources:
   159→  - name: data_lake
   160→    meta:
   161→      external_location: "r2://data-lake-bucket/{name}.parquet"
   162→    tables:
   163→      - name: events
   164→      - name: users
   165→      - name: transactions
   166→```
   167→
   168→### DuckDB vs Snowflake/Databricks の使い分け
   169→
   170→| 判断基準 | DuckDB (メイン) | Snowflake/Databricks (リカバリ) |
   171→|---------|----------------|-------------------------------|
   172→| データ規模 | 数 GB〜数十 GB | 数十 GB 以上、ペタバイト級 |
   173→| 同時接続 | シングルプロセス、少人数向け | 数十〜数百の同時クエリ |
   174→| コスト | 無料 (R2 ストレージ料金のみ) | 従量課金 |
   175→| ガバナンス | なし | RBAC、監査ログ、リネージ |
   176→| Lightdash 対応 | PR 開発中 (未マージ) | 完全サポート |
   177→| 切り替え契機 | デフォルト | クエリタイムアウト、データ量超過、チーム拡大時 |
   178→
   179→### MotherDuck という選択肢
   180→
   181→DuckDB のクラウド版。同時接続やスケーラビリティの課題を緩和する:
   182→
   183→```yaml
   184→# profiles.yml (MotherDuck)
   185→default:
   186→  outputs:
   187→    dev:
   188→      type: duckdb
   189→      path: "md:my_database"
   190→  target: dev
   191→```
   192→
   193→- Lightdash の DuckDB サポートがマージされれば MotherDuck も使える可能性がある
   194→- R2 の Parquet を MotherDuck にアタッチして分散クエリも可能
   195→
   196→## なぜ PostgreSQL だけ外部か
   197→
   198→Cloudflare には **フル PostgreSQL を提供するサービスがない**:
   199→
   200→- **D1** — SQLite ベース。Graphile Worker は PostgreSQL の `LISTEN/NOTIFY` に依存
   201→- **Hyperdrive** — 既存 PG へのプロキシ。PG インスタンス自体ではない
   202→
   203→## Supabase 設定のポイント
   204→
   205→### プラン選定
   206→
   207→| プラン | 一時停止 | ストレージ | 判断 |
   208→|-------|---------|-----------|------|
   209→| **Free** | 7日無活動で一時停止 | 500 MB | **検証・個人利用**。Graphile Worker が活動とみなされれば停止しない可能性あり |
   210→| Pro ($25/mo) | デフォルト OFF | 8 GB | 本番向け。一時停止の心配なし |
   211→| Team ($599/mo) | なし | 8 GB | エンタープライズ |
   212→
   213→> **Free プランの一時停止条件**: 7日間 DB への活動がないと一時停止される。
   214→> Graphile Worker の `LISTEN/NOTIFY` 常時接続が「活動」とみなされるかは**保証されていない**。
   215→> 週1回以上ダッシュボードにアクセスすれば実質的にリセットされる。
   216→
   217→### 接続方式
   218→
   219→| 種類 | 用途 | LISTEN/NOTIFY |
   220→|------|------|---------------|
   221→| **Direct 接続** (推奨) | Graphile Worker の長時間 TCP 接続 | 動作する |
   222→| Supavisor (Pooler) | 短命クエリ向け | Transaction mode では動作しない |
   223→
   224→Lightdash は `PGHOST` を 1 つしか取らないため、**Direct 接続を使う**。
   225→
   226→Supabase の Direct 接続情報は Settings > Database で確認:
   227→- Host: `db.<project-ref>.supabase.co`
   228→- Port: `5432`
   229→- Database: `postgres`
   230→
   231→### リージョン
   232→
   233→**Northeast Asia (Tokyo)** を選択。Cloudflare の東京エッジとの往復レイテンシを最小化する。
   234→
   235→### Neon ではなく Supabase を選んだ理由
   236→
   237→| 判断基準 | Supabase | Neon |
   238→|---------|----------|------|
   239→| Free の一時停止 | 7日無活動 (活動があれば停止しない) | 5分で強制 |
   240→| Free で Lightdash 継続運用 | 可能性あり (週1アクセスで維持) | 実質不可 |
   241→| 本番最小コスト | $25/月 (Pro) | $19/月 (Launch) |
   242→| 追加機能 | Auth, Storage, Edge Functions | PG 特化 |
   243→
   244→## 環境変数
   245→
   246→```env
   247→# --- Supabase PostgreSQL (Direct 接続) ---
   248→PGHOST=db.<project-ref>.supabase.co
   249→PGPORT=5432
   250→PGUSER=postgres
   251→PGPASSWORD=<supabase-db-password>
   252→PGDATABASE=postgres
   253→PGSSLMODE=require
   254→
   255→# --- Cloudflare R2 ---
   256→S3_ENDPOINT=https://<account-id>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com
   257→REDACTED
   258→S3_REGION=auto
   259→S3_ACCESS_KEY=<r2-access-key>
   260→S3_SECRET_KEY=<r2-secret-key>
   261→
   262→# --- Lightdash ---
   263→LIGHTDASH_SECRET=<random-secret>
   264→SCHEDULER_ENABLED=true
   265→```
   266→
   267→## dbt との接続
   268→
   269→2 つの方式がある:
   270→
   271→1. **`lightdash deploy` CLI** — CI/CD パイプラインから manifest.json を API 経由でアップロード（推奨）
   272→2. **ボリュームマウント** — dbt プロジェクトを `/usr/app/dbt` にマウント
   273→
   274→## Lightdash の内部パイプライン
   275→
   276→```mermaid
   277→graph LR
   278→    M[dbt manifest.json] -->|1. パース| V[バリデーション]
   279→    V -->|2. 変換| T[Model → Table<br/>ディメンション/メトリクス生成]
   280→    T -->|3. 組み立て| E[Explore 構築<br/>テーブル + JOIN]
   281→    E -->|4. SQL 解決| Q[実行可能 SQL 生成]
   282→    Q -->|クエリ実行| WH[データウェアハウス]
   283→```
   284→
   285→## スケジューラー (Graphile Worker)
   286→
   287→```mermaid
   288→graph LR
   289→    subgraph PG["Supabase PostgreSQL"]
   290→        JQ[ジョブキュー<br/>graphile_worker.jobs]
   291→    end
   292→
   293→    subgraph SCHED["Scheduler Worker"]
   294→        GW[Graphile Worker<br/>LISTEN/NOTIFY ポーリング]
   295→    end
   296→
   297→    GW -->|ジョブ取得| JQ
   298→    GW -->|HANDLE_SCHEDULED_DELIVERY| Slack[Slack 通知]
   299→    GW -->|SEND_EMAIL_NOTIFICATION| Email[メール通知]
   300→    GW -->|DOWNLOAD_CSV| R2[R2 保存]
   301→    GW -->|UPLOAD_GSHEETS| GS[Google Sheets]
   302→```
   303→
   304→ジョブ優先度:
   305→- **HIGH** — UI でユーザーが待っているジョブ
   306→- **MEDIUM** — 関連ジョブ
   307→- **LOW** — バックグラウンドジョブ
   308→
   309→## 認証オプション
   310→
   311→| プロバイダ | 環境変数プレフィックス |
   312→|-----------|---------------------|
   313→| Google OAuth | `AUTH_GOOGLE_*` |
   314→| Okta | `AUTH_OKTA_*` |
   315→| Azure AD | `AUTH_AZURE_AD_*` |
   316→| OneLogin | `AUTH_ONE_LOGIN_*` |
   317→| OIDC (汎用) | `AUTH_OIDC_*` |
   318→
   319→## 対応データウェアハウス
   320→
   321→| ウェアハウス | サポート状況 | この構成での役割 |
   322→|------------|------------|----------------|
   323→| **DuckDB** | PR 開発中 (#19866) | メインウェアハウス (予定) |
   324→| **PostgreSQL** | 完全サポート | 暫定ウェアハウス + メタデータ DB |
   325→| **Snowflake** | 完全サポート | リカバリ用 |
   326→| **Databricks** | 完全サポート | リカバリ用 |
   327→| BigQuery | 完全サポート | 未使用 |
   328→| Trino | 完全サポート | 未使用 |
   329→| Redshift | 完全サポート | 未使用 |
   330→| ClickHouse | 完全サポート | 未使用 |
   331→
   332→## ロードマップ
   333→
   334→1. **Phase 1 (現在)**: Supabase PostgreSQL をウェアハウス兼用。R2 に Parquet を蓄積開始
   335→2. **Phase 2 (DuckDB PR マージ後)**: dbt-duckdb が R2 Parquet → ローカル `.duckdb` にマテリアライズ、Lightdash が `.duckdb` に接続。Supabase はメタデータ専用に
   336→3. **Phase 3 (スケール時)**: MotherDuck または Snowflake/Databricks へ段階的移行
   337→
   338→## 関連ノート
   339→
   340→- [[Evidence]]
   341→- [[Cloudflare Data Platform]]
   342→- [[データアプリ構成：Evidence・Marimo・Streamlit]]
   343→- [[DuckDB WASM × R2 サーバーレス日本語全文検索]]
   344→

> AGENT

読みました。よく整理されたアーキテクチャドキュメントですね。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Lightdash は **Graphile Worker** が PostgreSQL の `LISTEN/NOTIFY` に依存するため、D1 (SQLite) では代替不可。Supabase の Direct 接続が必須
- DuckDB サポートは PR #19866 がまだ Open なので、**Phase 1 は Supabase PostgreSQL をウェアハウス兼用**する二段構成
- Cloudflare Containers でホストすることで、R2 との通信がクラウドフレア内部ネットワークで完結しレイテンシが最小化される
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

構成を整理すると:

**すぐやること (Phase 1)**
1. **Supabase** でプロジェクト作成 (Tokyo リージョン、Free プラン)
2. **R2 バケット** `lightdash-exports` 作成
3. **Cloudflare Containers** で `lightdash/lightdash:latest` をデプロイ
4. dbt manifest.json を `lightdash deploy` で同期
5. Supabase PostgreSQL をウェアハウス兼用

**主な判断ポイント**がいくつかあります:

1. **Supabase Free プランの一時停止リスク** — Graphile Worker の `LISTEN/NOTIFY` が「活動」とみなされるか未保証。週1回のアクセスで回避できるが、本番では $25/月の Pro が安全
2. **Headless Browser コンテナ** — スケジュール配信 (PDF/Slack通知) を使わないなら省略可能。最初は省略してコスト削減できる
3. **DuckDB PR #19866 の追跡** — マージされたら Phase 2 に移行し、Supabase をメタデータ専用に切り替え

どこから着手しますか？例えば:
- **A)** Supabase プロジェクト作成 + R2 バケット作成から
- **B)** Cloudflare Containers のデプロイ設定 (`containers.json` / Dockerfile) から
- **C)** まず全体の実装計画を Linear Issue として整理してから

```
     1→{{ config(location=r2_location('fct_orders')) }}
     2→with orders as (
     3→    select * from {{ ref('stg_orders') }}
     4→),
     5→
     6→order_items as (
     7→    select * from {{ ref('stg_order_items') }}
     8→),
     9→
    10→products as (
    11→    select * from {{ ref('stg_products') }}
    12→),
    13→
    14→order_totals as (
    15→    select
    16→        oi.order_id,
    17→        count(*) as total_items,
    18→        sum(oi.quantity) as total_quantity,
    19→        sum(oi.line_total) as gross_amount,
    20→        sum(p.cost * oi.quantity) as total_cost
    21→
    22→    from order_items oi
    23→    inner join products p on oi.product_id = p.product_id
    24→    group by 1
    25→),
    26→
    27→final as (
    28→    select
    29→        -- Primary Key
    30→        o.order_id,
    31→
    32→        -- Foreign Keys (Dimensions)
    33→        o.user_id,
    34→        o.order_date,
    35→
    36→        -- Order Attributes
    37→        o.status,
    38→        o.payment_method,
    39→        o.shipping_country,
    40→
    41→        -- Measures
    42→        ot.total_items,
    43→        ot.total_quantity,
    44→        ot.gross_amount,
    45→        o.discount_amount,
    46→        ot.gross_amount - o.discount_amount as net_amount,
    47→        ot.total_cost,
    48→        ot.gross_amount - o.discount_amount - ot.total_cost as profit,
    49→
    50→        -- Flags
    51→        case when o.status = 'completed' then true else false end as is_completed,
    52→        case when o.status = 'cancelled' then true else false end as is_cancelled,
    53→        case when o.status = 'refunded' then true else false end as is_refunded,
    54→
    55→        -- Metadata
    56→        current_timestamp as updated_at
    57→
    58→    from orders o
    59→    inner join order_totals ot on o.order_id = ot.order_id
    60→)
    61→
    62→select * from final
    63→
```

     1→{{ config(location=r2_location('dim_dates')) }}
     2→with date_spine as (
     3→    {{ dbt_utils.date_spine(
     4→        datepart="day",
     5→        start_date="cast('" ~ var('dim_dates_start_date', '2024-01-01') ~ "' as date)",
     6→        end_date="cast('" ~ var('dim_dates_end_date', '2027-01-01') ~ "' as date)"
     7→    ) }}
     8→),
     9→
    10→final as (
    11→    select
    12→        -- Primary Key
    13→        date_day,
    14→
    15→        -- Date Attributes
    16→        extract(year from date_day) as year,
    17→        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,
    18→        extract(month from date_day) as month,
    19→        extract(week from date_day) as week_of_year,
    20→        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,
    21→        extract(dow from date_day) as day_of_week,
    22→
    23→        -- Formatted Strings
    24→        {% if target.type == 'snowflake' %}
    25→        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY-MM') as year_month,
    26→        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY') || '-Q' || to_char(date_day, 'Q') as year_quarter,
    27→        to_char(date_day, 'MMMM') as month_name,
    28→        to_char(date_day, 'DAY') as day_name,
    29→        {% elif target.type == 'duckdb' %}
    30→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,
    31→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,
    32→        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,
    33→        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,
    34→        {% else %}
    35→        {{ exceptions.raise_compiler_error("Unsupported target type for dim_dates: " ~ target.type) }}
    36→        {% endif %}
    37→
    38→        -- Flags
    39→        case when extract(dow from date_day) in (0, 6) then true else false end as is_weekend,
    40→        case when extract(dow from date_day) between 1 and 5 then true else false end as is_weekday,
    41→
    42→        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)
    43→        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,
    44→        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter
    45→
    46→    from date_spine
    47→)
    48→
    49→select * from final
    50→

> AGENT

現状が把握できました。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- transform/core は **DuckDB + R2 Parquet** がメイン。staging は seed (CSV) を `ref()` で参照、marts は `r2_location()` マクロで R2 に Parquet 外部マテリアライズ
- `dim_dates.sql` には既に `target.type == 'snowflake'` の分岐がある — **マルチアダプター対応の意識がある**
- marts の materialized は `{{ 'external' if target.name == 'ci' else 'table' }}` — Databricks では `table` になるので問題なし
- `r2_location()` マクロは DuckDB の `external` materialization 専用。Databricks では不要（無視させる必要あり）
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## 移行に必要な変更点

Databricks で動かすには、いくつかの判断が必要です:

### 1. SQL 互換性の問題

| 箇所 | DuckDB | Databricks (Spark SQL) | 対応 |
|------|--------|----------------------|------|
| `dim_dates.sql` の `strftime` | DuckDB 関数 | `date_format` に置換 | `target.type` 分岐追加 |
| `extract(dow from ...)` | 0=Sunday | `dayofweek()` は 1=Sunday | 値のズレ注意 |
| `r2_location()` の `config(location=...)` | external materialization | 不要 | `target.type != 'databricks'` で条件分岐 |
| `current_timestamp` | 互換あり | OK | そのまま |
| `cast(... as date)` | 互換あり | OK | そのまま |

### 2. あなたの判断が必要なポイント

**A) Databricks のカタログ・スキーマ構成**
- transform/fusion は `production.marts` を使っていたが、core も同じカタログ？
- 例: `development.core_staging` / `development.core_marts` ？

**B) データソースの扱い**
- 現在は **CSV seeds** がソースデータ。Databricks でも `dbt seed` で CSV をロードする？
- それとも R2 Parquet を Databricks から直接読む外部テーブル化する？

**C) profiles.yml の接続方式**
- 既存の Warehouse ID `643b66d8a22ac512` を使う？
- 認証は Personal Access Token ？ OAuth (M2M) ？

方針を教えてもらえれば、profiles.yml と SQL の修正に進みます。

> DEVELOPER

完全にDatabrickｓに寄せて。

> AGENT

残りの SQL モデルも確認して、全体の変更を把握します。

     1→{{ config(location=r2_location('fct_daily_sales')) }}
     2→with fct_orders as (
     3→    select * from {{ ref('fct_orders') }}
     4→),
     5→
     6→fct_order_items as (
     7→    select * from {{ ref('fct_order_items') }}
     8→),
     9→
    10→daily_order_metrics as (
    11→    select
    12→        order_date,
    13→        count(*) as total_orders,
    14→        count(case when is_completed then 1 end) as completed_orders,
    15→        count(case when is_cancelled then 1 end) as cancelled_orders,
    16→        count(case when is_refunded then 1 end) as refunded_orders,
    17→        count(distinct user_id) as unique_customers,
    18→        sum(gross_amount) as gross_revenue,
    19→        sum(discount_amount) as total_discounts,
    20→        sum(net_amount) as net_revenue,
    21→        sum(total_cost) as total_cost,
    22→        sum(profit) as total_profit
    23→
    24→    from fct_orders
    25→    group by 1
    26→),
    27→
    28→daily_item_metrics as (
    29→    select
    30→        order_date,
    31→        count(*) as total_items_sold,
    32→        sum(quantity) as total_units_sold,
    33→        count(distinct product_id) as unique_products_sold,
    34→        count(distinct product_category) as unique_categories
    35→
    36→    from fct_order_items
    37→    where is_completed
    38→    group by 1
    39→),
    40→
    41→final as (
    42→    select
    43→        -- Primary Key
    44→        dom.order_date,
    45→
    46→        -- Order Metrics
    47→        dom.total_orders,
    48→        dom.completed_orders,
    49→        dom.cancelled_orders,
    50→        dom.refunded_orders,
    51→        dom.unique_customers,
    52→
    53→        -- Item Metrics
    54→        coalesce(dim.total_items_sold, 0) as total_items_sold,
    55→        coalesce(dim.total_units_sold, 0) as total_units_sold,
    56→        coalesce(dim.unique_products_sold, 0) as unique_products_sold,
    57→        coalesce(dim.unique_categories, 0) as unique_categories,
    58→
    59→        -- Revenue Metrics
    60→        dom.gross_revenue,
    61→        dom.total_discounts,
    62→        dom.net_revenue,
    63→        dom.total_cost,
    64→        dom.total_profit,
    65→
    66→        -- Calculated Metrics
    67→        round(dom.net_revenue * 1.0 / nullif(dom.completed_orders, 0), 2) as avg_order_value,
    68→        round(dom.total_profit * 100.0 / nullif(dom.net_revenue, 0), 2) as profit_margin_percent,
    69→
    70→        -- Metadata
    71→        current_timestamp as updated_at
    72→
    73→    from daily_order_metrics dom
    74→    left join daily_item_metrics dim on dom.order_date = dim.order_date
    75→)
    76→
    77→select * from final
    78→order by order_date
    79→
```

     1→{{ config(location=r2_location('fct_order_items')) }}

    38→        p.cost as unit_cost,
    39→        p.cost * oi.quantity as total_cost,
    40→        oi.line_total - (p.cost * oi.quantity) as profit,
    41→

    44→

    47→
    48→        -- Metadata
    49→        current_timestamp as updated_at
    50→
    51→    from order_items oi
    52→    inner join orders o on oi.order_id = o.order_id
    53→    inner join products p on oi.product_id = p.product_id
    54→)
    55→
    56→select * from final

     1→{{ config(location=r2_location('dim_products')) }}
     2→with products as (
     3→    select * from {{ ref('stg_products') }}
     4→),
     5→
     6→order_items as (
     7→    select * from {{ ref('stg_order_items') }}
     8→),
     9→
    10→orders as (
    11→    select * from {{ ref('stg_orders') }}
    12→),
    13→
    14→product_sales as (
    15→    select
    16→        oi.product_id,
    17→        count(distinct oi.order_id) as total_orders,
    18→        count(distinct case when o.status = 'completed' then oi.order_id end) as completed_orders,
    19→        sum(oi.quantity) as total_quantity_sold,
    20→        sum(case when o.status = 'completed' then oi.quantity else 0 end) as completed_quantity_sold,
    21→        sum(oi.line_total) as total_revenue,
    22→        sum(case when o.status = 'completed' then oi.line_total else 0 end) as completed_revenue
    23→
    24→    from order_items oi
    25→    inner join orders o on oi.order_id = o.order_id
    26→    group by 1
    27→),
    28→
    29→final as (
    30→    select
    31→        -- Primary Key
    32→        p.product_id,
    33→
    34→        -- Product Attributes
    35→        p.product_name,
    36→        p.category,
    37→        p.subcategory,
    38→        p.price,
    39→        p.cost,
    40→        p.margin,
    41→        round(p.margin * 100.0 / nullif(p.price, 0), 2) as margin_percent,
    42→        p.is_active,
    43→
    44→        -- Sales Metrics
    45→        coalesce(ps.total_orders, 0) as total_orders,
    46→        coalesce(ps.completed_orders, 0) as completed_orders,
    47→        coalesce(ps.total_quantity_sold, 0) as total_quantity_sold,
    48→        coalesce(ps.completed_quantity_sold, 0) as completed_quantity_sold,
    49→        coalesce(ps.total_revenue, 0) as total_revenue,
    50→        coalesce(ps.completed_revenue, 0) as completed_revenue,
    51→
    52→        -- Product Performance
    53→        case
    54→            when ps.completed_revenue >= 30000 then 'Top Performer'
    55→            when ps.completed_revenue >= 10000 then 'Good Performer'
    56→            when ps.completed_revenue > 0 then 'Low Performer'
    57→            else 'No Sales'
    58→        end as performance_tier,
    59→
    60→        -- Metadata
    61→        current_timestamp as updated_at
    62→
    63→    from products p
    64→    left join product_sales ps on p.product_id = ps.product_id
    65→)
    66→
    67→select * from final
    68→

    12→),
    13→

    16→        o.user_id,

    22→    from orders o

    34→    group by 1
    35→),

    48→

    51→        coalesce(uo.completed_orders, 0) as completed_orders,
    52→        coalesce(ur.total_revenue, 0) as total_revenue,
    53→        coalesce(ur.completed_revenue, 0) as completed_revenue,
    54→        uo.first_order_date,
    55→        uo.last_order_date,
    56→
    57→        -- Customer Segmentation
    58→        case
    59→            when ur.completed_revenue >= 50000 then 'High Value'
    60→            when ur.completed_revenue >= 20000 then 'Medium Value'
    61→            when ur.completed_revenue > 0 then 'Low Value'
    62→            else 'No Purchase'
    63→        end as customer_segment,
    64→
    65→        -- Metadata
    66→        current_timestamp as updated_at
    67→
    68→    from users u
    69→    left join user_orders uo on u.user_id = uo.user_id
    70→    left join user_revenue ur on u.user_id = ur.user_id
    71→)
    72→
    73→select * from final

> AGENT

全モデルを確認しました。変更箇所を整理して一気に進めます。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- staging モデル (4つ) は標準 SQL のみ — **変更不要**
- marts モデル (6つ) のうち、DuckDB 固有は `config(location=r2_location(...))` と `dim_dates.sql` の `strftime` / `extract(dow)` だけ
- Databricks (Spark SQL) では `strftime` → `date_format`、`extract(dow)` → `dayofweek()` に置換が必要
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

tool_result
id: toolu_01RZnbWYGAwvZfy34AdeeGaR
```
     1→{{ config(location=r2_location('dim_dates')) }}
     2→with date_spine as (
     3→    {{ dbt_utils.date_spine(
     4→        datepart="day",
     5→        start_date="cast('" ~ var('dim_dates_start_date', '2024-01-01') ~ "' as date)",
     6→        end_date="cast('" ~ var('dim_dates_end_date', '2027-01-01') ~ "' as date)"
     7→    ) }}
     8→),
     9→
    10→final as (
    11→    select
    12→        -- Primary Key
    13→        date_day,
    14→
    15→        -- Date Attributes
    16→        extract(year from date_day) as year,
    17→        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,
    18→        extract(month from date_day) as month,
    19→        extract(week from date_day) as week_of_year,
    20→        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,
    21→        extract(dow from date_day) as day_of_week,
    22→
    23→        -- Formatted Strings
    24→        {% if target.type == 'snowflake' %}
    25→        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY-MM') as year_month,
    26→        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY') || '-Q' || to_char(date_day, 'Q') as year_quarter,
    27→        to_char(date_day, 'MMMM') as month_name,
    28→        to_char(date_day, 'DAY') as day_name,
    29→        {% elif target.type == 'duckdb' %}
    30→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,
    31→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,
    32→        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,
    33→        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,
    34→        {% else %}
    35→        {{ exceptions.raise_compiler_error("Unsupported target type for dim_dates: " ~ target.type) }}
    36→        {% endif %}
    37→
    38→        -- Flags
    39→        case when extract(dow from date_day) in (0, 6) then true else false end as is_weekend,
    40→        case when extract(dow from date_day) between 1 and 5 then true else false end as is_weekday,
    41→
    42→        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)
    43→        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,
    44→        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter
    45→
    46→    from date_spine
    47→)
    48→
    49→select * from final
    50→
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql",
  "content": "with date_spine as (\n    {{ dbt_utils.date_spine(\n        datepart=\"day\",\n        start_date=\"cast('\" ~ var('dim_dates_start_date', '2024-01-01') ~ \"' as date)\",\n        end_date=\"cast('\" ~ var('dim_dates_end_date', '2027-01-01') ~ \"' as date)\"\n    ) }}\n),\n\nfinal as (\n    select\n        -- Primary Key\n        date_day,\n\n        -- Date Attributes\n        extract(year from date_day) as year,\n        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,\n        extract(month from date_day) as month,\n        weekofyear(date_day) as week_of_year,\n        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,\n        dayofweek(date_day) as day_of_week,\n\n        -- Formatted Strings\n        date_format(date_day, 'yyyy-MM') as year_month,\n        date_format(date_day, 'yyyy') || '-Q' || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,\n        date_format(date_day, 'MMMM') as month_name,\n        date_format(date_day, 'EEEE') as day_name,\n\n        -- Flags\n        case when dayofweek(date_day) in (1, 7) then true else false end as is_weekend,\n        case when dayofweek(date_day) between 2 and 6 then true else false end as is_weekday,\n\n        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)\n        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,\n        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter\n\n    from date_spine\n)\n\nselect * from final\n"
}
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Databricks の `dayofweek()` は **1=Sunday, 7=Saturday**。DuckDB の `extract(dow)` は **0=Sunday, 6=Saturday**
- そのため `is_weekend` の判定値が `(0, 6)` → `(1, 7)` に、`is_weekday` が `1..5` → `2..6` に変わる
- `date_format` のパターンは Java の `SimpleDateFormat` 準拠: `MMMM` = 月名、`EEEE` = 曜日名
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```
     1→---
     2→tags:
     3→  - dbt
     4→  - Cloudflare
     5→  - データエンジニアリング
     6→created: 2026-03-17
     7→---
     8→# dbt を Cloudflare 環境で動かす
     9→
    10→## 前提
    11→
    12→dbt は ELT の **T（Transform）** を担うツールで、DWH に SQL を送って変換を実行する。Cloudflare には従来の DWH がないため、「Cloudflare のどの部分で dbt を使うか」を整理する必要がある。
    13→
    14→## 3つのアプローチ
    15→
    16→```mermaid
    17→graph TD
    18→    subgraph A["A: CF でインジェスト、外部 DWH で変換"]
    19→        A1[Pipelines] --> A2[R2 Data Catalog<br/>Iceberg]
    20→        A2 -->|Iceberg REST API| A3[Snowflake / Databricks<br/>dbt run]
    21→    end
    22→    subgraph B["B: dbt を Containers で実行"]
    23→        B1[Cron Trigger] --> B2[Workers]
    24→        B2 --> B3[Container<br/>dbt Core Docker]
    25→        B3 -->|SQL| B4[外部 DWH]
    26→    end
    27→    subgraph C["C: 全て CF 内で完結（将来）"]
    28→        C1[Pipelines] --> C2[R2 Data Catalog]
    29→        C2 --> C3[R2 SQL<br/>dbt-r2sql?]
    30→    end
    31→```
    32→
    33→### A: Cloudflare でインジェスト、外部 DWH で dbt run（今すぐ実用的）
    34→
    35→最も現実的。Cloudflare は EL を担い、T は既存の DWH で行う。
    36→
    37→```
    38→データソース → Pipelines → R2 (Iceberg) → Snowflake Iceberg Table → dbt run
    39→```
    40→
    41→| 層 | 担当 | 製品 |
    42→|---|------|------|
    43→| Extract/Load | Cloudflare | Pipelines → R2 Data Catalog |
    44→| Storage | Cloudflare | R2（エグレスゼロ） |
    45→| Transform | 外部 DWH | Snowflake / Databricks + dbt |
    46→| アドホッククエリ | Cloudflare | R2 SQL |
    47→
    48→**Snowflake から R2 の Iceberg テーブルを参照する設定例:**
    49→
    50→```sql
    51→-- Snowflake で R2 の Iceberg カタログを参照
    52→CREATE OR REPLACE CATALOG INTEGRATION r2_catalog
    53→  CATALOG_SOURCE = ICEBERG_REST
    54→  TABLE_FORMAT = ICEBERG
    55→  CATALOG_URI = '<R2 Catalog URI>'
    56→  WAREHOUSE = '<warehouse_name>'
    57→  CATALOG_NAMESPACE = 'default'
    58→  ENABLED = TRUE;
    59→
    60→-- Iceberg テーブルを作成（R2 のデータを参照）
    61→CREATE ICEBERG TABLE raw.events
    62→  CATALOG = 'r2_catalog'
    63→  EXTERNAL_VOLUME = 'r2_vol'
    64→  CATALOG_TABLE_NAME = 'events';
    65→```
    66→
    67→```sql
    68→-- dbt model から通常通り source で参照
    69→-- models/staging/stg_events.sql
    70→SELECT * FROM {{ source('r2', 'events') }}
    71→WHERE event_date >= current_date - 7
    72→```
    73→
    74→**メリット**: エグレスゼロで R2 → Snowflake にデータを読み出せるため、ストレージコスト大幅削減。
    75→
    76→### B: dbt Core を Cloudflare Containers で実行（実験的）
    77→
    78→dbt Core の実行環境を Cloudflare 上に置く。ターゲット DWH は外部。
    79→
    80→```
    81→Cron Trigger → Workers → Container（dbt Docker）→ 外部 DWH
    82→```
    83→
    84→```dockerfile
    85→# dbt on Cloudflare Containers
    86→FROM ghcr.io/dbt-labs/dbt-snowflake:latest
    87→COPY dbt_project/ /app/
    88→WORKDIR /app
    89→CMD ["dbt", "run", "--profiles-dir", "/app/profiles"]
    90→```
    91→
    92→**メリット**: dbt の公式 Docker イメージをそのまま使える。CI/CD を Cloudflare 内で完結させたい場合に有用。
    93→
    94→**制約**: Containers はまだベータ。本番なら GitHub Actions + dbt Cloud の方が安定。
    95→
    96→### C: R2 SQL を dbt ターゲットにする（将来構想）
    97→
    98→R2 SQL が DDL + JOIN + CTE をサポートすれば dbt アダプタが書ける。
    99→
   100→**現在の R2 SQL で足りないもの:**
   101→
   102→| dbt が必要とする機能 | R2 SQL 対応状況 |
   103→|-------------------|----------------|
   104→| CREATE TABLE AS SELECT | 未対応 |
   105→| CREATE VIEW | 未対応 |
   106→| DROP TABLE / VIEW | 未対応 |
   107→| JOIN | 未対応 |
   108→| CTE (WITH句) | 未対応 |
   109→| GROUP BY / 集約 | 対応済み |
   110→| WHERE フィルタ | 対応済み |
   111→
   112→> R2 SQL は現時点では dbt ターゲットになれない。DDL と JOIN/CTE が最低限必要。
   113→
   114→## なぜ Containers を使うか
   115→
   116→dbt は信頼済みコードをバッチ実行するワークロードであり、Containers が最適。
   117→
   118→| 理由 | 詳細 |
   119→|------|------|
   120→| **Docker イメージ** | dbt + dbt-duckdb を Dockerfile で固定、再現性が高い |
   121→| **R2 バインディング** | マウントされた R2 から直接 read_parquet、S3 API トークン不要 |
   122→| **artifacts 保存** | Worker が R2 バインディングで直接 PUT、大きな manifest.json も問題なし |
   123→| **依存関係** | イメージに含まれるため毎回インストール不要 |
   124→| **実行時間** | 長時間の dbt run に耐えられる |
   125→
   126→## 推奨構成
   127→
   128→```mermaid
   129→graph LR
   130→    subgraph CF["Cloudflare"]
   131→        S[データソース] --> P[Pipelines]
   132→        P --> R2[R2 Data Catalog<br/>Iceberg]
   133→        R2 --> SQL[R2 SQL<br/>アドホック]
   134→        R2 --> W[Workers + DuckDB-WASM<br/>軽量API]
   135→    end
   136→    subgraph DWH["外部 DWH"]
   137→        R2 -->|Iceberg REST API<br/>エグレスゼロ| SF[Snowflake]
   138→        SF --> dbt[dbt run]
   139→        dbt --> mart[marts]
   140→        mart --> BI[BI ツール]
   141→    end
   142→```
   143→
   144→| 役割 | 推奨 | 理由 |
   145→|------|------|------|
   146→| インジェスト | Cloudflare Pipelines | ストリーミング取り込み、エグレスゼロ |
   147→| ストレージ | R2 Data Catalog (Iceberg) | $0.015/GB、オープンフォーマット |
   148→| 変換 (dbt) | Snowflake / Databricks | フルSQL、dbt アダプタ成熟 |
   149→| dbt 実行環境 | GitHub Actions / dbt Cloud | 安定性重視。実験的なら Containers |
   150→| アドホッククエリ | R2 SQL | フィルタ・集約はこれで十分 |
   151→| 分析 API | Workers + DuckDB-WASM | 軽量リクエスト向け |
   152→
   153→## 関連リンク
   154→
   155→- [[notes/Cloudflare Data Platform]]
   156→- [[dbt Python modelのユースケース]]
   157→- [[R2 Data Catalog - S3 Tablesとの比較と運用戦略]]
   158→- [[DuckDBの限界 - Snowflake・Databricksとの比較]]
   159→- [[サーバーレス比較 - Workers・Lambda・Cloud Functions・Azure Functions]]
   160→
```

> AGENT

ノートを確認しました。Cloudflare Containers で dbt を実行して Databricks に接続する構成ですね。

現在の `profiles.yml` に必要な環境変数は **3つ** です:

| 環境変数 | 値 | 取得方法 |
|---------|---|---------|
| `DATABRICKS_HOST` | `dbc-f6e7e914-ce58.cloud.databricks.com` | 既知（メモリから） |
| `DATABRICKS_HTTP_PATH` | `/sql/1.0/warehouses/643b66d8a22ac512` | SQL Warehouse の HTTP Path |
| `DATABRICKS_TOKEN` | `dapi...` | Personal Access Token |

`HTTP_PATH` のフォーマットについて補足:

```
# SQL Warehouse の場合
/sql/1.0/warehouses/<warehouse-id>

# All-Purpose Cluster の場合
/sql/1.0/endpoints/<cluster-id>
```

Warehouse ID `643b66d8a22ac512` は既に分かっているので、`HTTP_PATH` は `/sql/1.0/warehouses/643b66d8a22ac512` になります。

あとは Databricks 側で `core` カタログと `staging` / `marts` スキーマの作成が必要です:

```sql
CREATE CATALOG IF NOT EXISTS core;
CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS core.staging;
CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS core.marts;
```

これを Databricks で実行しますか？MCP で API を叩くこともできますし、あなたの方で Databricks UI から実行しても OK です。

Databricks 側の準備完了です:

- `core` カタログ
- `core.staging` スキーマ

変更のまとめ:

| ファイル | 変更内容 |
|---------|---------|
| `profiles.yml` | DuckDB/Snowflake → Databricks (dev/prod) |
| `pyproject.toml` | `dbt-duckdb` + `dbt-snowflake` → `dbt-databricks` |
| `dbt_project.yml` | marts の `external` materialization → `table` |
| `dim_dates.sql` | `strftime`/`extract(dow)` → `date_format`/`dayofweek()` |
| 5 marts models | `config(location=r2_location(...))` 削除 |
| `macros/r2_location.sql` | 削除 |

`dbt seed` → `dbt run` → `dbt test` を試しますか？まず `uv sync` で依存関係をインストールする必要があります。

> DEVELOPER

試して。

> AGENT

全10モデル成功。ただしスキーマ名が `staging_marts` / `staging_staging` になっています。これは profiles.yml のデフォルト schema が `staging` で、dbt_project.yml の `+schema: marts` が連結されているためです。修正します。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- dbt-databricks のスキーマ解決: `{default_schema}_{custom_schema}` が Databricks のデフォルト動作
- `core.staging` / `core.marts` にしたいなら、`generate_schema_name` マクロをオーバーライドして custom_schema をそのまま使う必要がある
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01LxTuR2FMyCgThg7BV8FFGq
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/core/macros/generate_schema_name.sql",
  "content": "{# Use custom_schema directly instead of default_{custom_schema} pattern #}\n{% macro generate_schema_name(custom_schema_name, node) -%}\n    {%- if custom_schema_name is not none -%}\n        {{ custom_schema_name | trim }}\n    {%- else -%}\n        {{ target.schema | trim }}\n    {%- endif -%}\n{%- endmacro %}\n"
}
```

}

> AGENT

完了です。結果まとめ:

| ステップ | 結果 |
|---------|------|
| `dbt seed` | **PASS 5** — `core.staging` に CSV ロード |
| `dbt run` | **PASS 10** — staging 4 views + marts 6 tables (`core.staging` / `core.marts`) |
| `dbt test` | **PASS 65** — 41 data tests + 24 unit tests |
| 旧スキーマ削除 | `staging_staging` / `staging_marts` を DROP 済み |

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- `generate_schema_name` マクロは dbt の重要なカスタマイズポイント。デフォルトの `{default}_{custom}` 連結は Databricks の Unity Catalog と相性が悪いので、custom_schema をそのまま使うオーバーライドが定番パターン
- dbt-databricks は unit test も完全サポート — DuckDB からの移行で SQL 互換性の問題がなかったのは、ビジネスロジック (staging/marts) が標準 SQL で書かれていたため
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

<title></title>
<text>
#### Configuration  
You can simply configure your Worker and your Container(s) in your Wrangler configuration file:  
   * [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-250)  
   * [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-251)  
```  
{  
  "name": "container-starter",  
  "main": "src/index.js",  
  "containers": [  
    {  
      "class_name": "MyContainer",  
      "image": "./Dockerfile",  
      "instances": 5  
    }  
  ],  
  "durable_objects": {  
    "bindings": [  
      {  
        "class_name": "MyContainer",  
        "name": "MY_CONTAINER"  
      }  
    ]  
  },  
  "migrations": [  
    {  
      "new_sqlite_classes": [  
        "MyContainer"  
      ],  
      "tag": "v1"  
    }  
  ],  
}  
```  
```  
name = "container-starter"  
main = "src/index.js"  
[[containers]]  
class_name = "MyContainer"  
image = "./Dockerfile"  
instances = 5  
[[durable_objects.bindings]]  
class_name = "MyContainer"  
name = "MY_CONTAINER"  
[[migrations]]  
new_sqlite_classes = [ "MyContainer" ]  
tag = "v1"  
```  
#### Worker Code  
Once your Worker and Containers are configured, you can access the Container instances from your Worker code:  
TypeScript  
```  
import { Container, getContainer } from "@cloudflare/containers";  
export class MyContainer extends Container {

### Worker code

#### Container Configuration

First note `MyContainer` which extends the [Container ↗](https://github.com/cloudflare/containers) class:

JavaScript

```

export class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 8080;

  sleepAfter = '10s';

  envVars = {

    MESSAGE: 'I was passed in via the container class!',

  };


  override onStart() {

    console.log('Container successfully started');

  }


  override onStop() {

    console.log('Container successfully shut down');

  }


  override onError(error: unknown) {

    console.log('Container error:', error);

  }

}


```

This defines basic configuration for the container:

* `defaultPort` sets the port that the `fetch` and `containerFetch` methods will use to communicate with the container. It also blocks requests until the container is listening on this port.
* `sleepAfter` sets the timeout for the container to sleep after it has been idle for a certain amount of time.
* `envVars` sets environment variables that will be passed to the container when it starts.
* `onStart`, `onStop`, and `onError` are hooks that run when the container starts, stops, or errors, respectively.

See the [Container class documentation](/containers/container-package) for more details and configuration options.

#### Routing to Containers

When a request enters Cloudflare, your Worker's [fetch handler](/workers/runtime-apis/handlers/fetch/) is invoked. This is the code that handles the incoming request. The fetch handler in the example code, launches containers in two ways, on different routes:


### Configuration

Your [Wrangler configuration file](/workers/wrangler/configuration/) defines the configuration for both your Worker and your container:

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-4164)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-4165)

```

{

  "containers": [

    {

      "max_instances": 10,

      "class_name": "MyContainer",

      "image": "./Dockerfile",

    },

  ],

  "durable_objects": {

    "bindings": [

      {

        "name": "MY_CONTAINER",

        "class_name": "MyContainer",

      },

    ],

  },

  "migrations": [

    {

      "tag": "v1",

      "new_sqlite_classes": ["MyContainer"],

    },

  ],

}


```

```

[[containers]]

max_instances = 10

class_name = "MyContainer"

image = "./Dockerfile"


[[durable_objects.bindings]]

name = "MY_CONTAINER"

class_name = "MyContainer"


[[migrations]]

tag = "v1"

new_sqlite_classes = [ "MyContainer" ]


```

Important points about this config:

* `image` points to a Dockerfile, to a directory containing a Dockerfile, or to a fully qualified image reference such as `registry.cloudflare.com/<YOUR_ACCOUNT_ID>/<IMAGE>:<TAG>`.
* `class_name` must be a [Durable Object class name](/durable-objects/api/base/).
* `max_instances` declares the maximum number of simultaneously running container instances that will run.
* The Durable Object must use [new\_sqlite\_classes](/durable-objects/best-practices/access-durable-objects-storage/#create-sqlite-backed-durable-object-class) not `new_classes`.

### The Container Image

Your container image must be able to run on the `linux/amd64` architecture, but aside from that, has few limitations.


### The Container Image

Your container image must be able to run on the `linux/amd64` architecture, but aside from that, has few limitations.

In the example you just deployed, it is a simple Golang server that responds to requests on port 8080 using the `MESSAGE` environment variable that will be set in the Worker and an [auto-generated environment variable](/containers/platform-details/#environment-variables) `CLOUDFLARE_DEPLOYMENT_ID.`

```

func handler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {

  message := os.Getenv("MESSAGE")

  instanceId := os.Getenv("CLOUDFLARE_DEPLOYMENT_ID")


  fmt.Fprintf(w, "Hi, I'm a container and this is my message: %s, and my instance ID is: %s", message, instanceId)

}


```

Note

After deploying the example code, to deploy a different image, you can replace the provided image with one of your own.

### Worker code

#### Container Configuration

First note `MyContainer` which extends the [Container ↗](https://github.com/cloudflare/containers) class:

JavaScript

```

export class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 8080;

  sleepAfter = '10s';

  envVars = {


## Deploy your first Container

Run the following command to create and deploy a new Worker with a container, from the starter template:

* [  npm ](#tab-panel-4152)
* [  yarn ](#tab-panel-4153)
* [  pnpm ](#tab-panel-4154)

Terminal window

```

npm create cloudflare@latest -- --template=cloudflare/templates/containers-template


```

Terminal window

```

yarn create cloudflare --template=cloudflare/templates/containers-template


```

Terminal window

```

pnpm create cloudflare@latest --template=cloudflare/templates/containers-template


```

When you want to deploy a code change to either the Worker or Container code, you can run the following command using [Wrangler CLI](/workers/wrangler/):

* [  npm ](#tab-panel-4155)
* [  yarn ](#tab-panel-4156)
* [  pnpm ](#tab-panel-4157)

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler deploy


```

Terminal window

```

yarn wrangler deploy


```

Terminal window

```

pnpm wrangler deploy


```

When you run `wrangler deploy`, the following things happen:

* Wrangler builds your container image using Docker.
* Wrangler pushes your image to a [Container Image Registry](/containers/platform-details/image-management/) that is automatically integrated with your Cloudflare account.
* Wrangler deploys your Worker, and configures Cloudflare's network to be ready to spawn instances of your container

The build and push usually take the longest on the first deploy. Subsequent deploys are faster, because they [reuse cached image layers ↗](https://docs.docker.com/build/cache/).

Note

After you deploy your Worker for the first time, you will need to wait several minutes until it is ready to receive requests. Unlike Workers, Containers take a few minutes to be provisioned. During this time, requests are sent to the Worker, but calls to the Container will error.


## Understanding the Code

Now that you've deployed your first container, let's explain what is happening in your Worker's code, in your configuration file, in your container's code, and how requests are routed.

## Each Container is backed by its own Durable Object

Incoming requests are initially handled by the Worker, then passed to a container-enabled [Durable Object](/durable-objects). To simplify and reduce boilerplate code, Cloudflare provides a [Container class ↗](https://github.com/cloudflare/containers) as part of the `@cloudflare/containers` NPM package.

You don't have to be familiar with Durable Objects to use Containers, but it may be helpful to understand the basics.

Each Durable Object runs alongside an individual container instance, manages starting and stopping it, and can interact with the container through its ports. Containers will likely run near the Worker instance requesting them, but not necessarily. Refer to ["How Locations are Selected"](/containers/platform-details/#how-are-locations-are-selected)for details.

In a simple app, the Durable Object may just boot the container and proxy requests to it.

In a more complex app, having container-enabled Durable Objects allows you to route requests to individual stateful container instances, manage the container lifecycle, pass in custom starting commands and environment variables to containers, run hooks on container status changes, and more.

See the [documentation for Durable Object container methods](/durable-objects/api/container/) and the[Container class repository ↗](https://github.com/cloudflare/containers) for more details.

* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-4165)

```

{

  "containers": [

    {

      "max_instances": 10,

```

Note

First run builds the Docker container (2-3 minutes). Subsequent runs are much faster due to caching.

Test the endpoints:

Terminal window

```

# Execute Python code

curl http://localhost:8787/run


# File operations

curl http://localhost:8787/file


```

You should see JSON responses with the command output and file contents.

## 4\. Deploy to production

Deploy your Worker and container:

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler deploy


```

This will:

1. Build your container image using Docker
2. Push it to Cloudflare's Container Registry
3. Deploy your Worker globally

Wait for provisioning

After first deployment, wait 2-3 minutes before making requests. The Worker deploys immediately, but the container needs time to provision.

Check deployment status:

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler containers list


```

## 5\. Test your deployment

Visit your Worker URL (shown in deploy output):

Terminal window

```

# Replace with your actual URL

curl https://my-sandbox.YOUR_SUBDOMAIN.workers.dev/run


```

Your sandbox is now deployed and can execute code in isolated containers.

Preview URLs require custom domain

If you plan to expose ports from sandboxes (using `exposePort()` for preview URLs), you will need to set up a custom domain with wildcard DNS routing. The `.workers.dev` domain does not support the subdomain patterns required for preview URLs. See [Production Deployment](/sandbox/guides/production-deployment/) when you are ready to expose services.

> AGENT

既存のコンテナ設定はありません。新規で作ります。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Cloudflare Containers は **Durable Objects ベース**。Worker が Container クラスを持ち、`fetch` でリクエストをコンテナに転送する
- コンテナは `linux/amd64` 必須、Dockerfile を `wrangler.jsonc` の `image` で指定すると `wrangler deploy` 時に自動ビルド＆プッシュ
- dbt はバッチジョブなので、Worker → Container を起動 → dbt run → 結果返却 → `sleepAfter` でコンテナ停止 という流れが最適
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

{

  "content": "FROM python:3.12-slim AS builder\n\nWORKDIR /app\n\n# Install uv for fast dependency resolution\nCOPY --from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest /uv /usr/local/bin/uv\n\n# Copy dependency files first for layer caching\nCOPY core/pyproject.toml core/uv.lock ./\nRUN uv sync --no-dev --frozen\n\n# Copy dbt project\nCOPY core/dbt_project.yml core/packages.yml core/profiles.yml ./\nCOPY core/models/ ./models/\nCOPY core/macros/ ./macros/\nCOPY core/seeds/ ./seeds/\nCOPY core/tests/ ./tests/\nCOPY core/analyses/ ./analyses/\nCOPY core/snapshots/ ./snapshots/\n\n# Install dbt packages\nRUN uv run dbt deps\n\n# --- Runtime ---\nFROM python:3.12-slim\n\nWORKDIR /app\n\nCOPY --from=builder /app /app\n\n# Install uv in runtime\nCOPY --from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest /uv /usr/local/bin/uv\n\n# HTTP server to receive commands from Worker\nCOPY dbt-runner/server.py ./server.py\n\nEXPOSE 8080\n\nCMD [\"uv\", \"run\", \"python\", \"server.py\"]\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/dbt-runner/server.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Lightweight HTTP server for dbt commands inside Cloudflare Container.\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport glob\nfrom http.server import HTTPServer, BaseHTTPRequestHandler\n\n\nclass DbtHandler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n    def do_GET(self):\n        if self.path == \"/health\":\n            self._json_response(200, {\"status\": \"ok\"})\n        else:\n            self._json_response(404, {\"error\": \"not found\"})\n\n    def do_POST(self):\n        if self.path == \"/run\":\n            self._run_dbt(\"run\")\n        elif self.path == \"/seed\":\n            self._run_dbt(\"seed\")\n        elif self.path == \"/test\":\n            self._run_dbt(\"test\")\n        elif self.path == \"/build\":\n            self._run_dbt(\"build\")\n        elif self.path == \"/artifacts\":\n            self._get_artifacts()\n        else:\n            self._json_response(404, {\"error\": \"not found\"})\n\n    def _run_dbt(self, command: str):\n        content_length = int(self.headers.get(\"Content-Length\", 0))\n        body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(content_length)) if content_length > 0 else {}\n\n        target = body.get(\"target\", \"dev\")\n        select = body.get(\"select\", None)\n        full_refresh = body.get(\"full_refresh\", False)\n\n        cmd = [\"uv\", \"run\", \"dbt\", command, \"--target\", target, \"--profiles-dir\", \".\"]\n        if select:\n            cmd.extend([\"--select\", select])\n        if full_refresh:\n            cmd.append(\"--full-refresh\")\n\n        result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=\"/app\")\n\n        self._json_response(\n            200 if result.returncode == 0 else 500,\n            {\n                \"command\": command,\n                \"returncode\": result.returncode,\n                \"stdout\": result.stdout,\n                \"stderr\": result.stderr,\n            },\n        )\n\n    def _get_artifacts(self):\n        \"\"\"Return dbt artifacts (manifest.json, run_results.json, etc.).\"\"\"\n        artifacts = {}\n        artifact_files = [\n            \"target/manifest.json\",\n            \"target/run_results.json\",\n            \"target/catalog.json\",\n            \"target/sources.json\",\n        ]\n        for path in artifact_files:\n            full_path = os.path.join(\"/app\", path)\n            if os.path.exists(full_path):\n                with open(full_path) as f:\n                    artifacts[os.path.basename(path)] = json.load(f)\n\n        self._json_response(200, {\"artifacts\": list(artifacts.keys()), \"data\": artifacts})\n\n    def _json_response(self, status: int, body: dict):\n        self.send_response(status)\n        self.send_header(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n        self.end_headers()\n        self.wfile.write(json.dumps(body).encode())\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    port = int(os.environ.get(\"PORT\", \"8080\"))\n    server = HTTPServer((\"0.0.0.0\", port), DbtHandler)\n    print(f\"dbt-runner server listening on port {port}\")\n    server.serve_forever()\n"
}

# Set this to today's date

compatibility_date = "2026-03-31"


[vars]

API_HOST = "https://example.com"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "API_KEY"

Add secrets to a `.dev.vars.example` or `.env.example` file:

.dev.vars.example

```

COOKIE_SIGNING_KEY=my-secret # comment


```

And optionally, you can add a description for these bindings in your template's `package.json` to help users understand how to configure each value:

package.json

```

{

  "name": "my-worker",

  "private": true,

  "cloudflare": {

    "bindings": {

      "API_KEY": {

        "description": "Select your company's API key for connecting to the example service."

      },

      "COOKIE_SIGNING_KEY": {

        "description": "Generate a random string using `openssl rand -hex 32`."

      }

    }

  }

}


```

These secrets and environment variables will be presented to users in the dashboard as they deploy this template, allowing them to configure each value. Additional information about creating templates and Deploy to Cloudflare buttons can be found in [our documentation](/workers/platform/deploy-buttons/).

package.json

  "private": true,  
  "cloudflare": {  
    "bindings": {  
      "API_KEY": {  
        "description": "Select your company's API key for connecting to the example service."  
      },  
      "COOKIE_SIGNING_KEY": {  
        "description": "Generate a random string using `openssl rand -hex 32`."  
      }  
    }  
  }  
}  
```  
These secrets and environment variables will be presented to users in the dashboard as they deploy this template, allowing them to configure each value. ### 3\. Set R2 API credentials as secrets

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler secret put R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID

npx wrangler secret put R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY


```

Create an R2 API token in the [Cloudflare dashboard ↗](https://dash.cloudflare.com/) under **R2** \> **Overview** \> **Manage R2 API Tokens**. The token needs **Object Read & Write** permissions for your backup bucket.

The SDK uses these credentials to generate presigned URLs that allow the container to transfer backup archives directly to and from R2\. For a complete setup walkthrough, refer to the [backup and restore guide](/sandbox/guides/backup-restore/).

# Env Vars and Secrets

**Last reviewed:**  8 months ago 

Pass in environment variables and secrets to your container

Environment variables can be passed into a Container using the `envVars` field in the [Container](/containers/container-package) class, or by setting manually when the Container starts.

Secrets can be passed into a Container by using [Worker Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/)or the [Secret Store](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/), then passing them into the Container as environment variables.

KV values can be passed into a Container by using [Workers KV](/kv/), then reading the values and passing them into the Container as environment variables.

These examples show the various ways to pass in secrets, KV values, and environment variables. In each, we will be passing in:

* the variable `"ENV_VAR"` as a hard-coded environment variable
* the secret `"WORKER_SECRET"` as a secret from Worker Secrets
* the secret `"SECRET_STORE_SECRET"` as a secret from the Secret Store
* the value `"KV_VALUE"` as a value from Workers KV

In practice, you may just use one of the methods for storing secrets and data, but we will show all methods for completeness.

## Creating secrets and KV data

First, let's create the `"WORKER_SECRET"` secret in Worker Secrets:

* [  npm ](#tab-panel-2048)
* [  yarn ](#tab-panel-2049)
* [  pnpm ](#tab-panel-2050)

Terminal window

```

</text>

<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/secrets-store/integrations/workers/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
## 2\. Bind an account secret to your Worker

[Bindings](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/) allow your Worker to interact with resources on your Cloudflare account.

To bind an account secret to your Worker, you must have one of the following [roles within your Cloudflare account](/secrets-store/access-control/):

* Super Administrator
* Secrets Store Deployer

### Via Wrangler

1. Add a Secrets Store binding to your [Wrangler configuration file](/workers/wrangler/configuration/):  
   * `binding`: a descriptive name for your binding. This will be used in the Workers application when [accessing your secret on the env object](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/#3-access-the-secret-on-the-env-object).  
   * `store_id`: the corresponding Secrets Store ID where your account secret was created.  
   * `secret_name`: the unique secret name, defined when your account secret was created.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-6957)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-6958)

```

{

  "main": "./src/index.js",

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "<BINDING_VARIABLE>",

      "store_id": "<STORE_ID>",

      "secret_name": "<MY_SECRET_NAME>"

    }

  ]

}


```

```

main = "./src/index.js"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "<BINDING_VARIABLE>"

store_id = "<STORE_ID>"

secret_name = "<MY_SECRET_NAME>"


```

### Via Dashboard

{

  "r2_buckets": [

    {

      "binding": "MY_BUCKET",

# Env Vars and Secrets

**Last reviewed:**  8 months ago 

Pass in environment variables and secrets to your container

Environment variables can be passed into a Container using the `envVars` field in the [Container](/containers/container-package) class, or by setting manually when the Container starts.

Secrets can be passed into a Container by using [Worker Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/)or the [Secret Store](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/), then passing them into the Container as environment variables.

KV values can be passed into a Container by using [Workers KV](/kv/), then reading the values and passing them into the Container as environment variables.

These examples show the various ways to pass in secrets, KV values, and environment variables. In each, we will be passing in:

* the variable `"ENV_VAR"` as a hard-coded environment variable
* the secret `"WORKER_SECRET"` as a secret from Worker Secrets
* the secret `"SECRET_STORE_SECRET"` as a secret from the Secret Store
* the value `"KV_VALUE"` as a value from Workers KV

In practice, you may just use one of the methods for storing secrets and data, but we will show all methods for completeness.

## Creating secrets and KV data

First, let's create the `"WORKER_SECRET"` secret in Worker Secrets:

* [  npm ](#tab-panel-2048)
* [  yarn ](#tab-panel-2049)
* [  pnpm ](#tab-panel-2050)

Terminal window

```

nv-as-a-global

import { env } from "cloudflare:workers";

export class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 8080;

  sleepAfter = "10s";

  envVars = {

    WORKER_SECRET: env.WORKER_SECRET,

    ENV_VAR: env.ENV_VAR,

    // we can't set the secret store binding or KV values as defaults here, as getting their values is asynchronous

  };

}


```

Every instance of this `Container` will now have these variables and secrets set as environment variables when it launches.

## Setting environment variables per-instance

But what if you want to set environment variables on a per-instance basis?

In this case, use the `startAndWaitForPorts()` method to pass in environment variables for each instance.

JavaScript

```

export class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 8080;

  sleepAfter = "10s";

}


export default {

  async fetch(request, env) {

    if (new URL(request.url).pathname === "/launch-instances") {

      let instanceOne = env.MY_CONTAINER.getByName("foo");

      let instanceTwo = env.MY_CONTAINER.getByName("bar");


      // Each instance gets a different set of environment variables


      await instanceOne.startAndWaitForPorts({

        startOptions: {

          envVars: {

            ENV_VAR: env.ENV_VAR + "foo",

            WORKER_SECRET: env.WORKER_SECRET,

            SECRET_STORE_SECRET: await env.SECRET_STORE.get(),

            KV_VALUE: await env.DEMO_KV.get("KV_VALUE"),

          },

        },

      });


      await instanceTwo.startAndWaitForPorts({

        startOptions: {

          envVars: {

            ENV_VAR: env.ENV_VAR + "bar",

            WORKER_SECRET: env.WORKER_SECRET,

            SECRET_STORE_SECRET: await env.SECRET_STORE.get(),

            KV_VALUE: await env.DEMO_KV.get("KV_VALUE"),

            // You can also read different KV keys for different instances

            INSTANCE_CONFIG: await env.DEMO_KV.get("instance-bar-config"),

          },

        },

      });

      return new Response("Container instances launched");

    }



Hello from KV!'


```

Terminal window

```

pnpm wrangler kv key put --binding DEMO_KV KV_VALUE 'Hello from KV!'


```

For full details on how to create secrets, see the [Workers Secrets documentation](/workers/configuration/secrets/)and the [Secret Store documentation](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/). For KV setup, see the [Workers KV documentation](/kv/).

## Adding bindings

Next, we need to add bindings to access our secrets, KV values, and environment variables in Wrangler configuration.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-2063)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-2064)

```

{

  "name": "my-container-worker",

  "vars": {

    "ENV_VAR": "my-env-var"

  },

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "SECRET_STORE",

      "store_id": "demo",

      "secret_name": "SECRET_STORE_SECRET"

    }

  ],

  "kv_namespaces": [

    {

      "binding": "DEMO_KV",

      "id": "<your-kv-namespace-id>"

    }

  ]

  // rest of the configuration...

}


```

```

name = "my-container-worker"


[vars]

ENV_VAR = "my-env-var"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "SECRET_STORE"

store_id = "demo"

secret_name = "SECRET_STORE_SECRET"


[[kv_namespaces]]

binding = "DEMO_KV"

id = "<your-kv-namespace-id>"


```

Note that `"WORKER_SECRET"` does not need to be specified in the Wrangler config file, as it is automatically added to `env`.

Also note that we did not configure anything specific for environment variables, secrets, or KV values in the _container-related_ portion of the Wrangler configuration file.

## Using `envVars` on the Container class

Now, let's pass the env vars and secrets to our container using the `envVars` field in the `Container` class:

JavaScript

```

// https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/#importing-env-as-a-global

import { env } from "cloudflare:workers";

export class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 8080;

  sleepAfter = "10s";

  envVars = {

    WORKER_SECRET: env.WORKER_SECRET,

    ENV_VAR: env.ENV_VAR,

    // we can't set the se
</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/containers/faq/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
## How long can instances run for? What happens when a host server is shutdown?

Cloudflare will not actively shut off a container instance after a specific amount of time. If you do not set `sleepAfter` on your Container class, or stop the instance manually, it will continue to run unless its host server is restarted. This happens on an irregular cadence, but frequently enough where Cloudflare does not guarantee that any instance will run for any set period of time.

When a container instance is going to be shut down, it is sent a `SIGTERM` signal, and then a `SIGKILL` signal after 15 minutes. You should perform any necessary cleanup to ensure a graceful shutdown in this time. The container instance will be rebooted elsewhere shortly after this.

## How can I pass secrets to my container?

You can use [Worker Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) or the [Secrets Store](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/)to define secrets for your Workers.

Then you can pass these secrets to your Container using the `envVars` property:

JavaScript

```

class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 5000;

  envVars = {

    MY_SECRET: this.env.MY_SECRET,

  };

}


```

Or when starting a Container instance on a Durable Object:

JavaScript

```

this.ctx.container.start({

  env: {

    MY_SECRET: this.env.MY_SECRET,

  },

});


```

See [the Env Vars and Secrets Example](/containers/examples/env-vars-and-secrets/) for details.

## Can I run Docker inside a container (Docker-in-Docker)?

Yes. Use the `docker:dind-rootless` base image since Containers run without root privileges.

You must disable iptables when starting the Docker daemon because Containers do not support iptables manipulation:

Dockerfile

```


</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/containers/get-started/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
### Worker code

#### Container Configuration

First note `MyContainer` which extends the [Container ↗](https://github.com/cloudflare/containers) class:

JavaScript

```

export class MyContainer extends Container {

  defaultPort = 8080;

  sleepAfter = '10s';

  envVars = {

    MESSAGE: 'I was passed in via the container class!',

  };


  override onStart() {

    console.log('Container successfully started');

  }


  override onStop() {

    console.log('Container successfully shut down');

  }


  override onError(error: unknown) {

    console.log('Container error:', error);

  }

}


```

This defines basic configuration for the container:

* `defaultPort` sets the port that the `fetch` and `containerFetch` methods will use to communicate with the container. It also blocks requests until the container is listening on this port.
* `sleepAfter` sets the timeout for the container to sleep after it has been idle for a certain amount of time.
* `envVars` sets environment variables that will be passed to the container when it starts.
* `onStart`, `onStop`, and `onError` are hooks that run when the container starts, stops, or errors, respectively.

See the [Container class documentation](/containers/container-package) for more details and configuration options.

#### Routing to Containers

When a request enters Cloudflare, your Worker's [fetch handler](/workers/runtime-apis/handlers/fetch/) is invoked. This is the code that handles the incoming request. The fetch handler in the example code, launches containers in two ways, on different routes:


</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
.
* `max_instances` string optional  
   * The maximum number of concurrent container instances you want to run at any given moment. Stopped containers do not count towards this - you may have more container instances than this number overall, but only this many actively running containers at once. If a request to start a container will exceed this limit, that request will error.  
   * Defaults to 20.  
   * This value is only enforced when running in production on Cloudflare's network. This limit does not apply during local development, so you may run more instances than specified.
* `name` string optional  
   * The name of your container. Used as an identifier. This will default to a combination of your Worker name, the class name, and your environment.
* `image_build_context` string optional  
   * The build context of the application, by default it is the directory of `image`.
* `image_vars` Record<string, string> optional  
   * Build-time variables, equivalent to using `--build-arg` with `docker build`. If you want to provide environment variables to your container at _runtime_, you should [use secret bindings or envVars on the Container class](/containers/examples/env-vars-and-secrets/).
* `rollout_active_grace_period` number optional  
   * The minimum number of seconds to wait before an active container instance becomes eligible for updating during a [rollout](/containers/faq#how-do-container-updates-and-rollouts-work). At that point, the container will be sent at `SIGTERM` and still has 15 minutes to shut down before it is forcibly killed and updated.  
   * Defaults to `0`.
* `rollout_step_percentage` number | number\[\] optional  
   * Configures what percentage of instances should be updated at each step of a [rollout](/containers/faq#how-do-container-updates-and-rollouts-work).
</text>
</result>

# Environment variables and secrets

Warning

Do not use `vars` to store sensitive information in your Worker's Wrangler configuration file. Use secrets instead.

Put secrets for use in local development in either a `.dev.vars` file or a `.env` file, in the same directory as the Wrangler configuration file.

Choose to use either `.dev.vars` or `.env` but not both. If you define a `.dev.vars` file, then values in `.env` files will not be included in the `env` object during local development.

These files should be formatted using the [dotenv ↗](https://hexdocs.pm/dotenvy/dotenv-file-format.html) syntax. For example:

.dev.vars / .env

```

Copy page

# Secrets

## Background

Secrets are a type of binding that allow you to attach encrypted text values to your Worker. Secrets are used for storing sensitive information like API keys and auth tokens.

You can access secrets in your Worker code through:

* The [env parameter](/workers/runtime-apis/handlers/fetch/#parameters) passed to your Worker's [fetch event handler](/workers/runtime-apis/handlers/fetch/).
* Importing `env` from [cloudflare:workers](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/#importing-env-as-a-global) to access secrets from anywhere in your code.
* [process.env](/workers/configuration/environment-variables) in Workers that have [Node.js compatibility](/workers/runtime-apis/nodejs/) enabled.

## Access your secrets with Workers

Secrets can be accessed from Workers as you would any other [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/). For instance, given a `DB_CONNECTION_STRING` secret, you can access it in your Worker code through the `env` parameter:

index.js

```

<title></title>
<text>
Mar 11, 2025
1. ### [Access your Worker's environment variables from process.env](/changelog/post/2025-03-11-process-env-support/)  
[ Workers ](/workers/)  
You can now access [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/) and[secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) on [process.env](/workers/runtime-apis/nodejs/process/#processenv)when using the [nodejs\_compat compatibility flag](/workers/configuration/compatibility-flags/#nodejs-compatibility-flag).  
JavaScript  
```  
const apiClient = ApiClient.new({ apiKey: process.env.API_KEY });  
const LOG_LEVEL = process.env.LOG_LEVEL || "info";  
```  
In Node.js, environment variables are exposed via the global `process.env` object. Some libraries assume that this object will be populated, and many developers may be used to accessing variables in this way.  
Previously, the `process.env` object was always empty unless written to in Worker code. This could cause unexpected errors or friction when developing Workers using code previously written for Node.js.  
Now, [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/),[secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/), and [version metadata](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/version-metadata/)can all be accessed on `process.env`.
</text>
</result>

Mar 11, 2025
1. ### [Access your Worker's environment variables from process.env](/changelog/post/2025-03-11-process-env-support/)  
[ Workers ](/workers/)  
You can now access [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/) and[secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) on [process.env](/workers/runtime-apis/nodejs/process/#processenv)when using the [nodejs\_compat compatibility flag](/workers/configuration/compatibility-flags/#nodejs-compatibility-flag).  
JavaScript  
```  
const apiClient = ApiClient.new({ apiKey: process.env.API_KEY });  
const LOG_LEVEL = process.env.LOG_LEVEL || "info";  
```  
In Node.js, environment variables are exposed via the global `process.env` object. Some libraries assume that this object will be populated, and many developers may be used to accessing variables in this way.  
Previously, the `process.env` object was always empty unless written to in Worker code. This could cause unexpected errors or friction when developing Workers using code previously written for Node.js.  
Now, [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/),[secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/), and [version metadata](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/version-metadata/)can all be accessed on `process.env`.
</text>

> AGENT

ドキュメントを確認しました。Container の `envVars` で Worker secrets をコンテナに渡す設計で進めます。R2 バインディングは Worker 側で、Worker がコンテナからアーティファクトを取得して R2 に保存します。

Dockerfile のビルドコンテキストを修正し、Worker とコンフィグを書きます。

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_0181gQ345pjUDxqshD3Wi4Ca
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/dbt-runner/Dockerfile",
  "content": "FROM python:3.12-slim\n\nWORKDIR /app\n\n# Install uv\nCOPY --from=ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:latest /uv /usr/local/bin/uv\n\n# Copy dependency files first for layer caching\nCOPY core/pyproject.toml core/uv.lock ./\n\nRUN uv sync --no-dev --frozen\n\n# Copy dbt project\nCOPY core/dbt_project.yml core/packages.yml core/profiles.yml ./\nCOPY core/models/ ./models/\nCOPY core/macros/ ./macros/\nCOPY core/seeds/ ./seeds/\nCOPY core/tests/ ./tests/\nCOPY core/analyses/ ./analyses/\nCOPY core/snapshots/ ./snapshots/\n\n# Install dbt packages\nRUN uv run dbt deps\n\n# Copy HTTP server\nCOPY dbt-runner/server.py ./server.py\n\nEXPOSE 8080\n\nCMD [\"uv\", \"run\", \"python\", \"server.py\"]\n"
}
```

Worker コード (TypeScript) を作成します:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01PvjtJM8ShpeBukLADwrvfp
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/dbt-runner/src/index.ts",
  "content": "import { Container, getContainer } from \"@cloudflare/containers\";\n\ninterface Env {\n  DBT_CONTAINER: DurableObjectNamespace;\n  DBT_ARTIFACTS: R2Bucket;\n  DATABRICKS_HOST: string;\n  DATABRICKS_HTTP_PATH: string;\n  DATABRICKS_TOKEN: string;\n}\n\nexport class DbtContainer extends Container {\n  defaultPort = 8080;\n\n  // dbt run は数分かかるため、十分なアイドル時間を確保\n  sleepAfter = \"5m\";\n\n  override get envVars(): Record<string, string> {\n    return {\n      DATABRICKS_HOST: this.env.DATABRICKS_HOST,\n      DATABRICKS_HTTP_PATH: this.env.DATABRICKS_HTTP_PATH,\n      DATABRICKS_TOKEN: this.env.DATABRICKS_TOKEN,\n    };\n  }\n\n  override onStart() {\n    console.log(\"dbt-runner container started\");\n  }\n\n  override onStop() {\n    console.log(\"dbt-runner container stopped\");\n  }\n\n  override onError(error: unknown) {\n    console.error(\"dbt-runner container error:\", error);\n  }\n}\n\nexport default {\n  async fetch(request: Request, env: Env): Promise<Response> {\n    const url = new URL(request.url);\n    const path = url.pathname;\n\n    // Health check\n    if (path === \"/health\") {\n      return Response.json({ status: \"ok\" });\n    }\n\n    // Get or create container instance\n    const container = getContainer(env.DBT_CONTAINER, \"dbt-runner-main\");\n\n    // dbt commands: POST /run, /seed, /test, /build\n    if (request.method === \"POST\" && [\"/run\", \"/seed\", \"/test\", \"/build\"].includes(path)) {\n      const body = await request.json().catch(() => ({}));\n      const response = await container.fetch(`http://container${path}`, {\n        method: \"POST\",\n        headers: { \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n        body: JSON.stringify(body),\n      });\n      const result = await response.json() as Record<string, unknown>;\n\n      // dbt run/build の成功後、アーティファクトを R2 に保存\n      if ((path === \"/run\" || path === \"/build\") && result.returncode === 0) {\n        await saveArtifactsToR2(container, env.DBT_ARTIFACTS);\n      }\n\n      return Response.json(result);\n    }\n\n    // アーティファクト取得 (R2 から)\n    if (path.startsWith(\"/artifacts/\")) {\n      const key = path.replace(\"/artifacts/\", \"\");\n      const object = await env.DBT_ARTIFACTS.get(`dbt-core/${key}`);\n      if (!object) {\n        return Response.json({ error: \"not found\" }, { status: 404 });\n      }\n      return new Response(object.body, {\n        headers: { \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n      });\n    }\n\n    return Response.json({ error: \"not found\" }, { status: 404 });\n  },\n};\n\nasync function saveArtifactsToR2(container: Container, bucket: R2Bucket) {\n  try {\n    const response = await container.fetch(\"http://container/artifacts\", {\n      method: \"POST\",\n    });\n    const data = await response.json() as {\n      artifacts: string[];\n      data: Record<string, unknown>;\n    };\n\n    const timestamp = new Date().toISOString().replace(/[:.]/g, \"-\");\n\n    for (const [filename, content] of Object.entries(data.data)) {\n      // 最新版を保存\n      await bucket.put(`dbt-core/latest/${filename}`, JSON.stringify(content), {\n        httpMetadata: { contentType: \"application/json\" },\n      });\n\n      // タイムスタンプ付きアーカイブ\n      await bucket.put(`dbt-core/history/${timestamp}/${filename}`, JSON.stringify(content), {\n        httpMetadata: { contentType: \"application/json\" },\n      });\n    }\n\n    console.log(`Saved ${data.artifacts.length} artifacts to R2: ${data.artifacts.join(\", \")}`);\n  } catch (error) {\n    console.error(\"Failed to save artifacts to R2:\", error);\n  }\n}\n"
}
```
